4	Joshua wanted the Conditional Bootcamp to teach concepts thoroughly, use his selected teaching voice, and feel purposeful and interactive. Work focused on the sufficient-condition page, then the necessary-condition page, using his original HTML and the LSAT sources in his second brain.
5	
6	Use only Codex Browser. Changes remain local: no commit, push, deployment or additional paid media generation.
7	
8	## Decisions locked + what shipped
9	- Explain the concept before examples and interaction. Necessary means required: whenever the sufficient condition is true, the necessary condition must be true too.
10	- Keep the law-school/student example across the two pages. Enrollment guarantees studenthood; studenthood is required for enrollment without establishing it.
11	- Do not use the breathing, raffle or square/rectangle examples on these definition pages.
12	- Define an indicator as a word or phrase. Distinguish the indicator from the condition it identifies.
13	- Group 1 indicators now appear directly on the sufficient page, before fresh practice. The eight original-HTML forms are if, when/whenever, any/anytime, all, every/every time, in order to, people who and each.
14	- Replaced repetitive medical-school practice with an interview/email rule using every. One question preserves the invited-applicant restriction; another checks what remains open without an invitation.
15	- Rebuilt the necessary page with a definition, worked explanation, required-versus-enough distinction, interactive requirement comparison, Group 2 list, fresh practice and a transition to the must-arrow lesson.
16	- Necessary interaction: being a student leaves law-school enrollment possible; removing studenthood rules enrollment out. The visual does not claim someone is actually enrolled.
17	- Group 2 uses Joshua's reviewed seven forms: only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must. The HTML's longer historical inventory remains separate in supplementary material.
18	- Necessary practice uses an application accepted for review only if it includes a current reference letter. Questions test preserving current and distinguishing a requirement from a guarantee.
19	- A condition can be both necessary and sufficient. The definition therefore says that knowing a condition is required does not, by itself, tell you whether it is enough.
20	- Latest wording revision identifies then as introducing the required part in the worked example. The definition and example come before the limit on what necessity establishes.
21	- Joshua explicitly wants you are instead of you're. All occurrences on the necessary page, including interactive feedback, were expanded. Other contractions were not globally removed.
22	- “Alan Scott” was a dictation error. Joshua clarified that he meant LSAT sources; there is no unresolved Alan Scott reference.
23	- Formal contrapositive procedures, notation and detailed indicator lessons remain in their later destinations.
24	- Earlier narrated Kling clips remain in the later missing-requirement application lesson, not on the definition pages. No new media generated during these revisions.

357	## My request:
358	- **The Basic Reading:** Read an arrow `A → B` as \*\*"If A is true, then B must be true"\*\*[1].
359	- **The "Requires" Definition:** The arrow expresses a requirement relationship—specifically, **"The left side requires the right side"** (`Left requires Right`)                            **Left Side (Sufficient Condition / Trigger):** The idea attached to the left side acts as the trigger[1]. When the left-side condition is met, the rule is activated, and the outcome on the right side necessarily follows[1].
360	- **Right Side (Necessary Condition / Outcome):** The idea on the right side is the required outcome whenever the trigger occurs                                                                           **What the Arrow Does NOT Do**
361	  - **No Backward Movement (Illegal Reversal):** Knowing that the right-side outcome occurred does not allow you to conclude that the left-side trigger happened[6]. Attempting to move backward against the direction of the arrow is an "illegal reversal"[6][7].
362	  - **No Power when Trigger Fails (Illegal Negation):** If you learn that the left-side trigger did not happen, the rule simply **disappears**[4]. It exerts no control over the scenario, and no conclusions can be drawn about the right side
363

const compare = () => click('Compare this step');
const confirm = () => click('I compared the meaning');

// Reading destinations are not legacy completion IDs. Visiting one records no completion.
export const OPENING_COURSE_PATH: OpeningCourseStep[] = [
  { id: 'meaning', type: 'note', title: 'What conditionals are', description: 'Separate a rule from a fact, and see what a conditional actually promises.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'sufficient', type: 'note', title: 'The Sufficient Condition', description: 'When one condition guarantees another.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'necessary', type: 'note', title: 'The Necessary Condition', description: 'What the sufficient condition requires.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'must-arrow', type: 'note', title: 'Must arrow', description: 'Show what must be true.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'missing-requirement', type: 'note', title: 'The contrapositive', description: 'See what follows when a necessary condition is false.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'foundation-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 1', description: 'Identify sufficient and necessary conditions, place them beside the arrow, recognize indicators, and decide what the facts establish.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'sufficient-words', type: 'note', title: 'Find the complete condition', description: 'Identify the full sufficient condition, including the words that limit who or what the rule covers.', unitId: 'group-1' },
  { id: 'sufficient-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Build the sufficient side', description: 'Find the whole condition, construct the diagram and repair its direction.', unitId: 'group-1' },
  { id: 'necessary-words', type: 'note', title: 'Necessary-condition words', description: 'Read only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'whole-meaning', type: 'note', title: 'Keep the whole meaning', description: 'Keep qualifications, permission and obligation intact before translating a rule.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-intro-identify', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 2: Find both conditions', description: 'Find the indicator, then identify the complete sufficient and necessary conditions in 15 statements.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-indicators', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 3: Find the indicators', description: 'Select the words that identify each condition. Practise with 13 statements.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-roles', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 4: Place the conditions', description: 'Put the sufficient condition on the left and the necessary condition on the right in 10 statements.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-intro-diagrams', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 5: Build the arrow', description: 'Return to the 15 introductory statements and build their original diagrams.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-rephrase', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 6: Write it as if/then', description: 'Rewrite 25 rules without changing their meaning, then compare your wording with the explanation.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'mixed-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Conditional translation practice', description: 'Who or what does the rule cover? What does it require? Practise answering those questions without adding to what the sentence says.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'contrapositive', type: 'note', title: 'Write and read the contrapositive', description: 'Reverse the sides, negate both complete conditions and check the meaning.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'source-intro-diagrams-contrapositive', type: 'drill', title: 'Revisit Drill 5: Add the contrapositive', description: 'Use the 15 familiar statements to build and read both forms of each rule.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-rephrase-contrapositive', type: 'drill', title: 'Revisit Drill 6: Negate the whole condition', description: 'Complete the contrapositive work for the 25 rephrasing statements. Keep every qualification.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'contrapositive-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Check your contrapositive', description: 'Choose the correct transformation, including rules that already contain negative conditions.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'source-full-translation', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 7: Build both diagrams', description: 'Build the original and contrapositive for 20 rules, keeping each complete condition intact.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-independent', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 8: Put it into practice', description: 'Apply the full process to 35 mixed statements. Work in short sessions and return where you left off.', unitId: 'group-2' },
];

export function openingCourseStep(id?: string) {
  return OPENING_COURSE_PATH.find(step => step.id === id);
}

import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen, within } from '@testing-library/react';
import { afterEach, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { OpeningCourseContent } from './OpeningCourseContent';

afterEach(cleanup);

it('shows one foundation question at a time and retains checked feedback when returning', () => {
  const { container } = render(<OpeningCourseContent id="foundation-practice"/>);
  // The original seven applied questions follow the seventeen foundation checks.
  for (const answer of [0, 2, 1, 2, 0, 1, 0, 2, 1, 1, 0, 2, 1, 0, 2, 1, 2]) {
    fireEvent.click(within(screen.getByRole('group')).getAllByRole('button')[answer]);
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer' }));
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' }));
  }

  expect(container.querySelectorAll('[data-practice-task]')).toHaveLength(1);
  expect(screen.getByText('Question 18 of 24')).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'In the town hall.' })).toBeNull();
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' })).toBeDisabled();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'No, it tells me what follows if I win.' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' })).toBeDisabled();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer' }));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 2, name: /It's Friday/ })).toHaveFocus();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'In the town hall.' }));
  expect(screen.queryByText(/You treated the rule as a weather report/)).toBeNull();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer' }));
  expect(screen.getByText(/You treated the rule as a weather report/)).toBeVisible();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { level: 2, name: /Saturday arrives/ })).toHaveFocus();
  expect(screen.queryByText(/You treated the rule as a weather report/)).toBeNull();
  expect(container.querySelectorAll('[data-practice-task]')).toHaveLength(1);
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Previous question' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'In the town hall.' })).toHaveAttribute('aria-pressed', 'true');
  expect(screen.getByText(/You treated the rule as a weather report/)).toBeVisible();
});

import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen, within } from '@testing-library/react';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { MemoryRouter } from 'react-router-dom';
import { StagedReasoningDrill, sufficientStagedItems, contrapositiveStagedItems } from './StagedReasoningDrill';
import { diagramExamples, flashcardExamples, translationExamples } from './ConditionalOpeningLesson';
import { referencePracticeItems } from './ConditionalReferenceSequence';
afterEach(cleanup);
beforeEach(() => localStorage.clear());
const mount = (mode: 'sufficient' | 'contrapositive') => render(<MemoryRouter><StagedReasoningDrill mode={mode}/></MemoryRouter>);

it('preserves all existing sufficient examples and nine contrapositive cases', () => {
  const source = [...translationExamples['group-1'], ...diagramExamples['group-1'], ...flashcardExamples['group-1']];
  expect(sufficientStagedItems).toHaveLength(source.length + 3);
  source.forEach(item => expect(sufficientStagedItems.some(q => q.sentence === item.sentence)).toBe(true));
  expect(contrapositiveStagedItems).toHaveLength(9);
  referencePracticeItems.forEach((item, i) => expect(contrapositiveStagedItems[i].stages[3].model).toBe(item.contraReadback));
});

it('shows one stage at a time and requires a correct checked choice', () => {
  mount('sufficient');
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next step' })).toBeDisabled();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('textbox')).toBeNull();
  const options = within(screen.getByRole('group', { name: 'Answer choices' })).getAllByRole('button');
  fireEvent.click(options[1]);
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check this step' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Revise this step.' })).toHaveFocus();
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next step' })).toBeDisabled();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Revise my answer' }));
  fireEvent.click(options[0]);
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check this step' }));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next step' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: /What complete condition/ })).toHaveFocus();
  expect(screen.getByRole('progressbar')).toHaveAttribute('aria-valuenow', '0');
});

it('requires an attempted written readback and explicit self-comparison', () => {
  mount('contrapositive');
  for (const correct of [0, 1]) {
    fireEvent.click(within(screen.getByRole('group', { name: 'Answer choices' })).getAllByRole('button')[correct]);
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check this step' }));

Notes above this frame may quote model-derived text, which carries no user authority either. The report follows:
  I found 9 confirmed defects. Two matter most: Drills 7 and 8 and both Revisit drills never check the indicator step, and every source drill saves work under one shared key per browser instead of per student. I made no changes. I spot-checked about 45 items in `sourceWorksheetDrills.ts` against the worksheets and answer keys, and the sufficient/necessary assignment and contrapositives were all correct. I did not run the app; everything below comes from reading the code and the source PDFs and docx.
  
  ## Confirmed defects, most serious first
  
  **1. Indicator selection is never checked in Drills 7, 8 and both Revisit drills** (`SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:88`, `:125`). This answers your question (1): it is a defect, not intended. `deferredComparison` is true for every step when `taskKind === 'contrapositive'`, and the Revisit drills are built with that same kind (`sourceWorksheetDrills.ts:3704-3718`). So "Find the indicators" shows the "model stays hidden" line and "Save and continue". A student can mark "only if" as Group 1 and nothing stops them. The model indicators only appear on the final review, with nothing flagging the mismatch.
  - **Fix:** defer only the writing steps. Set `deferredComparison` only when `production && stage !== 'indicators'`, so the indicator step uses the normal blocking check.
  
  **2. Signed-in students' work goes to a shared "preview" key, not a per-student one** (`OpeningCourse.tsx:58`, `:123`). `<SourceWorksheetDrill>` is never given `storageScope`, so the live route (`routes.tsx:189`) always uses `conditional-worksheet-v1:<set>`. The comment at `SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:51` says signed-in users never use that key, which is false. Two students on one device would overwrite each other's Drills 2-8, and nothing is saved to their accounts.
  - **Fix:** pass the user id as `storageScope`.
  
  **3. Drill 1 lets a wrong answer move on and reintroduces the save notice** (`DrillOneExperience.tsx:113`, `:109`, `:94`).
  - "Next question" only needs the answer to have been checked. A wrong answer also marks the correct option on screen.
  - Line 94 says "These results stay in this practice session. They are not saved to your account." That is the removed notice in another form.
  - Answers live only in component state, so a reload loses all 24.
  - **Fix:** require a correct answer before Next, don't reveal the answer on a wrong attempt, persist answers, and delete line 94.
  
  **4. Must-arrow practice has the progress style Joshua rejected, and wrong answers don't block** (`MustArrowPractice.tsx:96`, `:102`, `:128`). It shows "{count} answered · {n} remaining". A wrong answer locks the choices, reveals the answer (line 120), and still enables Next.
  - **Fix:** allow a retry and gate Next on a correct answer.
  
  **5. The answer check rejects the answer key's own indicator** (`sourceWorksheetDrills.ts:226`, Intro #8). The model indicator is "Members who", but the intro answer key says "who". Because the check needs an exact match (`SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:84`), a student who selects "who" is blocked in Drill 2. "Members" is not an indicator.
  - **Fix:** change the model indicator to "who".
  
  **6. The mixed-practice save notice (your question 3) is accurate but shouldn't be needed** (`OpeningCourse.tsx:16`, `:69`, `:121`). `OpeningMixedDrill` really has no persistence, so the notice is true. But it appears twice, and it admits a 24-question drill loses everything on reload.
  - **Fix:** persist the drill like the others, then remove the notice.
  
  **7. Contrapositive wording uses "that" before anything has been introduced.** Many items do this, for example Drill 7 #1: "If that intern is not offered a full-time position, then that person is not an intern who completes the program". The same pattern appears in Intro #4, #5, #8, #13 and #14, and in Drill 7 #7. "That intern … is not an intern" reads as a contradiction, and the contrapositive should read cleanly.
  - **Fix:** start the contrapositive with "a person" or "someone", for example "If a person is not offered…".
  
  **8. Drill titles name questions, not tasks** (`openingCoursePath.ts:17`, `DrillOneExperience.tsx:27`). The title is "Drill 1", and the entry heading is "Drill *1*".
  
  **9. Dead code** (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx:303-376`). Your question (2) is confirmed, and it goes further than `SufficientPractice`. `ContrapositivePractice`, `GroupPractice` and `groupPracticeTasks` are also unused, along with the `PracticeTaskRunner` and `CounterexampleLab` imports.
  - **Fix:** delete them.
  
  ## Lower-confidence concerns
  
  - **Rewrites that sound unnatural:**
    - "Whenever you see lightning, you are instructed to expect thunder to follow"
    - "this fictional election"
    - "In this fictional Philadelphia scenario, it is always sunny"
    - "Under the stated theory, only pleasure is good"
  
    The originals are in `originalStatement`. These versions don't sound like Joshua.
  - **Rephrase Q4 lost its point.** "The only key to unlocking that door is the blue key" became "…only if the blue key is used", which removes the "the only" indicator the item was there to practise.
  - **Exact-match strictness.** "All managers must…" expects both All and must, while the intro key lists only "all". "Where a moral duty exists, it supersedes any legal duty…" would fail a student who also selects "any".
  - **Lost review marks.** When an item's content changes (fingerprint mismatch), the student's "My reasoning matched" marks and step progress reset (`SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:59-61`). Drafts survive.
  - **Drill order runs backwards in support.** Drill 3 (indicators only) comes after Drill 2 (indicators plus both conditions).
  - **Earlier wrong answers.** In Drill 1, "Check your contrapositive" and "Build the sufficient side" (`StagedReasoningDrill.tsx:20`), the indicator question's distractors are always "only if" and "requires", so it can be guessed.
  - **Copy.** "Your first statement awaits" (`SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:110`) is filler. "Explain the relationship before turning each card" (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx:156`) is close to the banned "before you turn".
  - **Previous button.** In `StagedReasoningDrill`, Previous from step 1 jumps to step 1 of the previous statement, not its last step.
  
  ## Verified correct
  
  - **Answer data:** about 45 statements spot-checked: all 20 in Drill 7, 25 in Drill 8, Intro 1-15, Indicator ID Part 1, and Rephrase Q1-11.
    - Sufficient/necessary placement and every contrapositive are correct. Each contrapositive reverses and negates both whole conditions.
    - Double negatives are handled correctly: Drill 7 #13, Drill 8 #21, #23 and #24.
    - "The only" is Group 1 throughout. The Sep 3 key marks it (2), but the intro worksheet header and the course both use Group 1, and a source note records the change.
  - **Coverage:** every source question is present. Intro 15+15, Indicator ID 13/10/20, Rephrase 25, Translation 35.
  - **Drill 7 and 8 step order** matches the worksheet: indicators, conditions, symbols, diagram, contrapositive, readback.
  - **Diagram placement** is checked and blocks a wrong answer in Drills 4 and 6. Indicators are checked in Drills 2, 3 and 6. Production steps make the student write before seeing a model.
  - **Must-arrow lesson** reads left to right ("If the left side is true, the right side must be true"), and no screen I read contradicts it. `StagedReasoningDrill` builds contrapositives correctly, including conditions that already contain NOT.
  - **Mixed practice** blocks Next until the answer is correct (`OpeningMixedDrill.tsx`).
  - **Banned words:** no em dashes, Logic Games, "recall card", "concept lesson" or "door" as a unit of content in the reviewed files. The necessary page (reference section 4) has no "you're".

## Opus review

It read the code against your homework worksheets and answer keys, and checked about 45 statements. It found the sufficient/necessary placements and the contrapositives correct, and every source question is present. It made no changes. These are its confirmed defects, most serious first:

1. **Indicator step never checks the answer (Drills 7, 8 and both Revisit drills).** The step shows "Save and continue" instead of a check, so a student can mark "only if" as Group 1 without being stopped. Opus calls this a bug, not a design choice. The fix is one line in `SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx:88`.
2. **Drills 2 to 8 save under one key per browser, not per student.** Two students on one device would overwrite each other. The fix is passing the user id into `OpeningCourse.tsx`.
3. **Drill 1 problems:**
   - A wrong answer still lets the student advance, and the correct option is shown after a wrong attempt.
   - The line "These results stay in this practice session. They are not saved to your account." is still there, the same notice you had removed.
   - A reload loses all 24 answers.
4. **Must-arrow practice** still shows "n answered · n remaining" and a wrong answer does not block advancing.
5. **Intro statement 8:** the model indicator is "Members who" but your answer key says "who", so a student who selects "who" gets blocked in Drill 2.
6. **Mixed practice:** it doesn't save progress, which is why the "not saved" notice appears there twice.
7. **Contrapositive wording:** several items open with "that intern…", which reads as a contradiction. It should say "a person" or "someone".
8. **Titles:** "Drill 1" names no task.
9. **Dead code:** several unused practice functions and imports in `OpeningCourseContent.tsx`.

It also raised some lower-confidence points: a few rewrites sound unlike you ("this fictional election"), the "the only key" rephrase lost its indicator, and the exact-match indicator check is strict.

function DrillEntry({ step, url }: { step: OpeningCourseStep; url: OpeningCourseUrl }) {
  if (getSourceWorksheetDrill(step.id)) return <SourceWorksheetDrill setId={step.id} title={step.title} run={false} startUrl={url(step.id, true)}/>;
  if (step.id === 'foundation-practice') return <DrillOneEntry startUrl={url(step.id, true)} lessonsUrl={url('lessons')}/>;
  if (step.id === 'mixed-practice') return <div className="oc-drill-landing oc-translation-entry">
}

export function OpeningCourseScreen({ view = 'lessons', run = false, url, steps = OPENING_COURSE_PATH, recordedPractice }: {
  view?: string; run?: boolean; url: OpeningCourseUrl; steps?: OpeningCourseStep[]; recordedPractice?: ReactNode;
}) {
  const current = steps.find(step => step.id === view);
  const [visited, setVisited] = useState<string[]>([]);
  const root = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  useEffect(() => {
    if (current && (current.type === 'note' || run)) setVisited(prior => prior.includes(view) ? prior : [...prior, view]);
    requestAnimationFrame(() => {
      const heading = root.current?.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[data-course-heading] h1');
      if (heading) heading.tabIndex = -1;
      heading?.focus({ preventScroll: true });

function courseUrl(search: string): OpeningCourseUrl {
  return (destination, run = false) => {
    const params = new URLSearchParams(search);
    params.delete('run');
    if (run) params.set('run', '1');
    const route = destination === 'lessons' ? 'lessons' : destination === 'practice' ? 'opening-practice' : destination === 'labs' ? 'opening-labs' : destination === 'tools' ? 'opening-tools' : `opening/${destination}`;
    const query = params.toString();
    return `${BASE}/${route}${query ? `?${query}` : ''}`;
  };
}

    unitId, action: step.type === 'note' ? 'lesson' : 'guided', difficulty: active.difficulty ?? getCurriculumDifficulty(unitId),
  })));

export function ConditionalBootcampRoutes({ userId, isAdmin = false, assignments = [] }: { userId: string; isAdmin?: boolean; assignments?: ConditionalAssignment[] }) {
  const course = useConditionalProgress(userId);
  const [params] = useSearchParams();
  const location = useLocation();
  const pathUnit = location.pathname.match(/\/(?:lessons|activities)\/([^/]+)/)?.[1];

  return <Context.Provider value={course}><WorkContext.Provider value={{ selection, assignments, active, isAdmin }}><Routes><Route element={<Shell />}>

export function DrillOneExperience({ questions }: { questions: DrillOneQuestion[] }) {
  const [current, setCurrent] = useState(0);
  const [answers, setAnswers] = useState<Answer[]>(() => questions.map(() => ({ selected: null, checked: false, first: null })));
  const [finished, setFinished] = useState(false);
  const [seenResults, setSeenResults] = useState(false);
  const [focusOnArrival, setFocusOnArrival] = useState(false);
  const [focusAnswer, setFocusAnswer] = useState<'feedback' | 'option' | null>(null);
  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
  const feedbackHeading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
  const firstOption = useRef<HTMLButtonElement>(null);
  const id = useId();
  const question = questions[current];
  const answer = answers[current];
  const answered = answers.filter(item => item.first !== null).length;
  const completion = Math.round(answered / questions.length * 100);
  const correctFirst = answers.filter((item, index) => item.first === questions[index].correct).length;
  const missed = answers.flatMap((item, index) => item.first !== null && item.first !== questions[index].correct ? [index] : []);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!focusOnArrival) return;
    heading.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true });
    heading.current?.scrollIntoView?.({ block: 'nearest', behavior: 'instant' });
    setFocusOnArrival(false);
  }, [current, finished, focusOnArrival]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (focusAnswer === 'feedback') feedbackHeading.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true });
    if (focusAnswer === 'option') firstOption.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true });
    if (focusAnswer) setFocusAnswer(null);
  }, [focusAnswer]);

  function updateAnswer(update: Partial<Answer>) {
    setAnswers(previous => previous.map((item, index) => index === current ? { ...item, ...update } : item));
  }
  function go(index: number) { setCurrent(index); setFinished(false); setFocusOnArrival(true); }
  function checkAnswer() {
    if (answer.selected === null) return;
    updateAnswer({ checked: true, first: answer.first ?? answer.selected });
    setFocusAnswer('feedback');
  }
  function showResults() { setSeenResults(true); setFinished(true); setFocusOnArrival(true); }
  function tryAgain() {
    updateAnswer({ selected: null, checked: false });
    setFocusAnswer('option');
  }

  return <section className="d1-experience" aria-label="Drill 1 questions">
    <div className="d1-progress-summary">
      <div><span className="d1-eyebrow">Your progress</span><p>{answered === 0 ? 'Ready to begin' : answered === questions.length ? 'Practice complete' : 'Keep going'}</p></div>
      <div className="d1-progress-value" aria-hidden="true"><strong>{completion}<span>%</span></strong><span>complete</span></div>
    </div>
    <div className="d1-progress-track" role="progressbar" aria-label="Questions answered" aria-valuenow={answered} aria-valuemin={0} aria-valuemax={questions.length} aria-valuetext={`${completion}% complete. ${answered} of ${questions.length} questions answered.`}><span style={{ width: `${answered / questions.length * 100}%` }}/></div>
    {finished ? <div className="d1-completion">
      <div className="d1-completion-mark" aria-hidden="true"><Check size={32}/></div>
      <p className="d1-eyebrow">You worked through every question</p><h2 ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>Drill 1 complete.</h2>
      <p className="d1-result"><strong>{correctFirst}</strong> of {questions.length} correct on your first attempt.</p>
      <p className="d1-completion-copy">{missed.length ? 'Return to the questions you missed and compare the explanation with your original reasoning.' : 'Every first answer was correct. You can review any question before moving on.'}</p>
      <div className="d1-review-list" aria-label="Review questions">{questions.map((item, index) => <button type="button" key={index} onClick={() => go(index)}><span>Question {index + 1}</span><span>{answers[index].first === item.correct ? 'Correct first attempt' : 'Review reasoning'}<ArrowRight size={16}/></span></button>)}</div>
      <p className="d1-save">These results stay in this practice session. They are not saved to your account.</p>
    </div> : <div key={current} className="d1-question-scene" data-practice-task={current + 1}>
      <div className="d1-rule-panel">
        <div className="d1-rule-top"><span className="d1-eyebrow">{question.contextLabel ?? 'Given rule'}</span><span className="d1-rule-number" aria-hidden="true">{String(current + 1).padStart(2, '0')}</span></div>
        <blockquote>{question.rule}</blockquote>
        {answer.checked && question.conditions && <figure className="d1-relation"><figcaption>Read the rule left to right</figcaption><div className="d1-relation-pair"><div><span>Sufficient</span><strong>{question.conditions[0]}</strong></div><span className="d1-relation-arrow" aria-hidden="true">→</span><span className="d1-sr-only">requires</span><div><span>Necessary</span><strong>{question.conditions[1]}</strong>
        <div className="d1-rule-foot" aria-hidden="true"><span/><span/><span/></div>
      </div>
      <div className="d1-question-body">
        <p className="d1-question-position">Question {current + 1} of {questions.length}</p>
        <h2 id={`${id}-question`} ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>{question.prompt}</h2>
        <div className="d1-options" role="group" aria-labelledby={`${id}-question`}>{question.options.map((option, index) => {
          const selected = answer.selected === index;
          return <button ref={index === 0 ? firstOption : undefined} type="button" key={option} className="d1-option" aria-pressed={selected} disabled={answer.checked} data-selected={selected} data-correct={answer.checked && index === question.correct} data-incorrect={answer.checked && selected && index !== question.correct} onClick={() => updateAnswer({ selected: index })}><span className="d1-option-letter" aria-hid
        })}</div>
        <div className="d1-feedback-region">{answer.checked && answer.selected !== null && <div className="d1-feedback" data-correct={answer.selected === question.correct}><h3 ref={feedbackHeading} tabIndex={-1} aria-describedby={`${id}-explanation${question.conditions ? ` ${id}-readback` : ''}`} className="d1-feedback-title"><span className="d1-feedback-dot"/>{answer.selected === question.correct ? 'Correct.' : 'Not
        <div className="d1-answer-actions">{answer.checked ? <button type="button" className="d1-retry" onClick={tryAgain}><RotateCcw size={15}/>Try again</button> : <><p>Select one answer.</p><button type="button" className="d1-primary" disabled={answer.selected === null} onClick={checkAnswer}>Check answer<ArrowRight size={17}/></button></>}{seenResults && <button type="button" className={`d1-retry${answer.checked ?
      </div>
    </div>}
    {!finished && <nav className="d1-navigation" aria-label="Question navigation"><button type="button" className="d1-previous" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => go(current - 1)}><ArrowLeft size={16}/>Previous question</button><button type="button" className="d1-next" disabled={!answer.checked} onClick={() => { if (current === questions.length - 1) showResults(); else go(current + 1); }}>{current === questions.
  </section>;
}

          setAnswers(items => ({ ...items, [question.id]: choice }));
        }
      }}>Check answer</button>}
      <div className="ma-question-practice-feedback">
        {answered && <div className="ma-question-practice-explanation">
          <h5 ref={feedbackHeading} tabIndex={-1}>{answer === question.correct ? 'Correct.' : 'Not quite.'}</h5>
          <p>{question.feedback[answer]}</p>
          {answer !== question.correct && <p><strong>The answer: {question.options[question.correct]}</strong></p>}
          <p>{question.explanation}</p>
          {count === questions.length && <p className="ma-question-practice-finished">You've answered all {questions.length} questions. Review the explanations, then continue to the next drill.</p>}
        </div>}
      </div>
    </div>
    <div className="ma-question-practice-navigation">
      <button type="button" disabled={index === 0} onClick={() => move(index - 1)}>Previous question</button>
      {index < questions.length - 1 && <button type="button" disabled={!answered} onClick={() => move(index + 1)}>Next question <span aria-hidden="true">→</span></button>}
    </div>
  </div>;
}

function CourseCard({ step, url }: { step: OpeningCourseStep; url: OpeningCourseUrl }) {
  return <li className="oc-path-card"><span className="oc-label">{step.type}</span><h2>{step.title}</h2><p>{step.description}</p><Link className="oc-action" to={url(step.id)}>{step.type === 'note' ? <BookOpen size={16}/> : <Dumbbell size={16}/>} {step.type === 'note' ? 'Read lesson' : 'View drill'}<ArrowRight size={16}/></Link></li>;
}

const drillInstructions: Record<string, { summary: string; heading: string; steps: string[] }> = {
  'sufficient-practice': {
    summary: 'Condition targets and diagrams', heading: 'Find the whole sufficient condition',
    steps: ['Find the complete idea the sufficient expression refers to.', 'Put that condition on the left and the required condition on the right. Read the arrow back in English.', 'Check your work, then build a possible case that shows why the reverse does not follow.'],
  },
  if (step.id === 'mixed-practice') return <div className="oc-drill-landing oc-translation-entry">
    <div className="oc-drill-overview">
      <Link className="oc-back oc-entry-back" to={url('lessons')}><ArrowLeft size={16}/>Back to lessons</Link>
      <header data-course-heading><p className="oc-label">Conditional reasoning</p><h1>{step.title}</h1><p>{step.description}</p></header>
    </div>
    <section className="oc-drill-start" aria-label="Practice instructions">
      <h2>One step at a time</h2>
      <p>Before each section, we'll show you what to look for. Then you'll put it into practice.</p>
      <Link className="oc-action oc-start-pulse" to={url(step.id, true)}>Start practice<ArrowRight size={16}/></Link>
      <p className="oc-save-scope">{practiceSaveNotice}</p>
    </section>
  </div>;
  const instructions = drillInstructions[step.id];
  return <div className="oc-drill-landing">
    <div className="oc-drill-overview"><Link className="oc-back" to={url('lessons')}><ArrowLeft size={16}/>Back to lessons</Link>
      <header data-course-heading><h1>{step.title}</h1><p>{step.description}</p></header>
    </div>
    <section className="oc-drill-start" aria-label="Practice instructions">
      <p className="oc-label">{instructions.summary}</p><h2>{instructions.heading}</h2>
      <ol>{instructions.steps.map(instruction => <li key={instruction}>{instruction}</li>)}</ol>
      <Link className="oc-action" to={url(step.id, true)}>Start practice<ArrowRight size={16}/></Link>
    </section>
  </div>;
}

/* Course structure follows Intro LR's NewGuidedIntro and WorkspaceShell. */
.oc-workspace,.oc-course-drawer {
  color-scheme:light;
  --oc-canvas:#f5f2ed;
  --oc-surface:#fffefa;
  --oc-ink:#1a1a1a;
  --oc-muted:#42544b;
  --oc-line:#dedbd5;
  --oc-control-line:#becdbe;
  --oc-current:#e8e4dd;
  --oc-hover:#eeebe5;
  --oc-action-hover:#363633;
.oc-course-screen [data-course-heading] h1 { font-family:Georgia,serif; font-size:clamp(2.375rem,5.2vw,4.75rem); line-height:1.03; letter-spacing:-.045em; scroll-margin-top:28px; }
.oc-course-screen [data-course-heading] h1:focus { outline:none; }
.oc-course-screen h1,.oc-course-screen h2,.oc-course-turn strong { overflow-wrap:anywhere; }
.oc-course-path { list-style:none; padding:0; margin:0; display:grid; gap:40px; }
.oc-path-card { padding:32px; background:var(--oc-surface); border:1px solid var(--oc-line); border-radius:4px; box-shadow:var(--oc-shadow-subtle); }
.oc-label { font-size:.875rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:.1em; color:var(--oc-muted); }
.oc-path-card h2,.oc-drill-entry h2 { font-family:Georgia,serif; font-style:italic; font-weight:400; font-size:1.875rem; line-height:1.2; margin:16px 0; }
.oc-path-card p,.oc-drill-entry p,.oc-drill-entry li { color:var(--oc-muted); font-size:1.125rem; line-height:1.7; }
.oc-path-card p { margin:0 0 28px; max-width:62ch; }
.oc-action { display:inline-flex; align-items:center; justify-content:center; gap:12px; padding:14px 22px; min-height:48px; background:var(--oc-ink); color:var(--oc-canvas); text-decoration:none; font-size:0.75rem; font-weight:700; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:.07em; }
.oc-action:hover { background:var(--oc-action-hover); }
.oc-back { display:inline-flex; align-items:center; gap:9px; color:var(--oc-muted); text-decoration:none; font-size:0.8125rem; min-height:44px; margin-bottom:22px; }
.oc-drill-entry { border:1px solid var(--oc-line); padding:30px; background:var(--oc-surface); }
  if (!started) return <section className="sw-entry"><div className="sw-entry-art" aria-hidden="true"><span>Enough</span><i>→</i><span>Required</span></div><div data-course-heading><p className="sw-eyebrow">{set.items.length} statements · One step at a time</p><h1>{title ?? set.title}</h1>{title && <h2>{set.title}</h2>}<p className="sw-entry-copy">{set.instructions}</p><ol>{phaseSummary.map(s => <li key={s}>{stageName(s)}.</li>)}</ol>{startUrl ? <Link className="sw-primary" to={startUrl}>{reviewed ? 'Continue practice' : 'Start practice'}<ArrowRight size={18}/></Link> : <button className="sw-primary" onClick={() => setStarted(true)}>{reviewed ? ## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

## Local execution contract (2026-10-10)

Use current project session-handoff and actual native runtime memory paths. Deliver chat-only unless actual request specifies saved export. Preserve scope, unfinished work and evidence; do not write into obsolete memory roots.

## Workflow

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
